I hope you don't think I'm doing things like that without realizing it. I'm usually very aware of what I'm doing. Is that the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you feel that need to shift around or move a bit, is it ever just a discomfort in your body that makes you want to move, even if there's nothing specific you're trying to fix or organize?\n\nPatient: Oh, just a general discomfort... well, I suppose... maybe? I mean, sometimes sitting in those wooden pews for a long time can make anyone's back a little stiff, don't you think? \n\nBut just a... a vague discomfort that makes me move for no reason? Oh, I don't think I'd call it that. That makes it sound like I'm restless in a bothersome way, and I'm really not! I'm quite content most of the time. If I shift, it's really just to get a little more comfortable, like anyone would. I'm sure you must adjust your posture in that chair of yours from time to time, right? \n\nI just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture. I wouldn't want you to write down that I'm uncomfortable in my own skin or anything silly like that. I'm just a normal older lady trying to stay comfortable. That's perfectly alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you feel that urge to move or shift around, even slightly, is there ever a moment where you feel like it's not about being comfortable or doing a task, but more like your body just doesn't feel right unless you're doing something, even if it's small?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness. I... I really don't think my body ever feels like it *has* to be doing something just to feel right. That sounds so... so demanding, doesn't it? Like I'm at the mercy of my own nerves! \n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes I just prefer to be busy. It's nice to feel useful, even if it's just a small thing like folding a napkin or smoothing the tablecloth. But it's not because I feel wrong if I don't do it. It's just a preference, really. A habit of a lifetime, you could say. \n\nI certainly don't feel driven by some... some inner compulsion that I can't ignore. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting at home feeling all out of sorts unless my hands are moving! I'm quite peaceful, truly. I hope that's the kind of answer you need? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right idea.\n\nDoctor: When you're completely still, like when you're lying in bed trying to fall asleep, do you ever feel like your body just wants to keep moving, even just a little, even if there's no reason to?\n\nPatient: Oh, lying in bed... well... no, not really. I mean, when it's time to sleep, I just close my eyes and try to rest. It would be quite awful to be lying there tossing and turning, feeling like I *had* to move, wouldn't it? I'm sure people who have that must be so exhausted. \n\nI suppose sometimes it takes a little while to settle in, but that's just because I'm thinking about the day or... or maybe my hip aches a tiny bit, which is perfectly normal at my age, you know. But I don't feel like my body is trying to keep moving on its own. I'm usually quite still once I'm tucked in. I wouldn't want you to think I'm thrashing about in the dark! I'm a very quiet sleeper, really. Does that sound normal to you? I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you ever notice any unusual sensations in your legs, like tingling, crawling, or an urge to move them, especially when you're sitting still or lying down?\n\nPatient: Oh, tingling or crawling? Oh my, no. That sounds terribly unpleasant, doesn't it? Like... like having bugs on you! No, I've never felt anything strange like that in my legs. \n\nI mean, sometimes my legs get a little heavy if I've been on my feet too long, perhaps doing a bit too much gardening or standing at the sink. But that's just a normal tiredness, I'm sure. It's certainly not an urge to move them when I'm trying to rest. When I sit down in my armchair with a cup of tea, my legs are very happy to just stay right where they are! \n\nI wouldn't want you to worry that I have some sort of... nervous condition in my legs. They're perfectly fine, just a bit older now. That's a good thing, isn't it? That I don't have those crawling feelings you mentioned? I do try to take good care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're trying to rest or relax, especially at night in bed, do you ever feel a strong urge to move your legs, even if you're not aware of any discomfort or specific reason to move them?\n\nPatient: Oh, I feel like I must be giving you the exact same answer again, I'm so sorry! But no, truly, I don't feel any sort of... strong urge to move them. \n\nWhen it's time to relax, my legs are just as happy to rest as the rest of me. I just lie there quite peacefully. I hope it's alright that I keep saying no to these questions? I'd hate for you to think I'm being difficult or, oh, not paying proper attention to my own body. But they really are very well-behaved at night, I promise you. Am I doing alright with these answers? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're resting in the evening or trying to fall asleep at night, do you ever feel a need to move your legs, even just slightly, even if there's no discomfort or specific reason to do so?\n\nPatient: Oh dear, we are back to the legs, aren't we? When I'm settled for the evening, perhaps watching a little television before bed, my legs are perfectly still. I don't get fidgety or... or restless. I just sit quietly, like a good lady should. \n\nI hope you aren't frustrated with me for saying no so many times. It's just that I've always prided myself on being quite calm and composed, even when resting. I'm not the sort to be twitching about. Is it... is it alright that I don't have this problem? I do hope I'm still being a helpful patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Even when there's absolutely nothing you need to do and no one is expecting anything from you, do you still feel like you should be doing something, even just a little bit, like it's hard to be at peace unless you're engaged in some kind of activity?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm just wiggling about for no reason at all! \n\nWhenever I do shift around or get up, there's always a little purpose to it, I think. Even if it's just to... well, to smooth a wrinkle in my skirt or fetch a peppermint from my purse. It's never just moving with absolutely nothing in mind. I always try to be quite deliberate, you know? \n\nI hope I'm explaining that properly... I just wouldn't want to give the impression that I'm out of control of my own body or doing things without a reason. That would be dreadful. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: When you feel that urge to move or fidget, are there times when you're not trying to accomplish anything specific, like adjusting your clothes or organizing something, but you just feel the need to move your hands or feet without a clear reason?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I really don't think so. I mean... just moving my hands or feet for absolutely no reason at all? That sounds a bit... well, a bit odd, doesn't it? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting there twitching or anything unseemly like that. \n\nIf I'm moving my foot, it's usually because it's fallen asleep, or maybe I'm just tapping along to a nice hymn in my head. And my hands... well, like I said, I like to keep them busy with my knitting or tidying. I don't think I ever just move them without knowing why. That would be quite strange, wouldn't it? I hope you don't think I'm doing things like that without realizing it. I'm usually very aware of what I'm doing. Is that the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you feel that need to shift around or move a bit, is it ever just a discomfort in your body that makes you want to move, even if there's nothing specific you're trying to fix or organize?\n\nPatient: Oh, just a general discomfort... well, I suppose... maybe? I mean, sometimes sitting in those wooden pews for a long time can make anyone's back a little stiff, don't you think? \n\nBut just a... a vague discomfort that makes me move for no reason? Oh, I don't think I'd call it that. That makes it sound like I'm restless in a bothersome way, and I'm really not! I'm quite content most of the time. If I shift, it's really just to get a little more comfortable, like anyone would. I'm sure you must adjust your posture in that chair of yours from time to time, right? \n\nI just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture. I wouldn't want you to write down that I'm uncomfortable in my own skin or anything silly like that. I'm just a normal older lady trying to stay comfortable. That's perfectly alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you feel that urge to move or shift around, even slightly, is there ever a moment where you feel like it's not about being comfortable or doing a task, but more like your body just doesn't feel right unless you're doing something, even if it's small?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness. A habit of a lifetime, you could say. \n\nI certainly don't feel driven by some... some inner compulsion that I can't ignore. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting at home feeling all out of sorts unless my hands are moving! I'm quite peaceful, truly. I hope that's the kind of answer you need? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right idea.\n\nDoctor: When you're completely still, like when you're lying in bed trying to fall asleep, do you ever feel like your body just wants to keep moving, even just a little, even if there's no reason to?\n\nPatient: Oh, lying in bed... well... no, not really. I mean, when it's time to sleep, I just close my eyes and try to rest. It would be quite awful to be lying there tossing and turning, feeling like I *had* to move, wouldn't it? I'm a very quiet sleeper, really. Does that sound normal to you? I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you ever notice any unusual sensations in your legs, like tingling, crawling, or an urge to move them, especially when you're sitting still or lying down?\n\nPatient: Oh, tingling or crawling? Oh dear, we are circling back to this, aren't we? I hope I'm not being difficult, Doctor. You asked about when I'm completely alone... and I suppose it happens then, but where I really notice it—and I feel a bit ashamed to admit this, because a woman my age should certainly know how to sit properly by now—is when I'm supposed to be sitting quietly with others.
